These innovations are crucial as LLMs scale to longer contexts and are expected to play a key role in next-generation models. Modern transformer-based LLM architectures have built-in support for KV caching to accelerate inference. In practice, KV caching is enabled by default in most decoder models. For instance, the Hugging Face Transformers library automatically caches past_key_values when use_cache=True, so each new decoding step concatenates the latest key/value with the previous cache instead of recomputing it. Below is a simplified PyTorch-style pseudocode illustrating this mechanism: ## Simplified KV cache usage in PyTorch if cache[&quot;key&quot;] is None: cache[&quot;key&quot;] = new_key # first token: initialize cache cache[&quot;\\n\\nIn summary, the theoretical advances aim to balance memory and compute: either by making the cache footprint smaller (through compression, quantization, or fixed windows) or by structuring the attention to not need every past token (via selective or sparse retrieval of KV). These innovations are crucial as LLMs scale to longer contexts and are expected to play a key role in next-generation models. Modern transformer-based LLM architectures have built-in support for KV caching to accelerate inference. In practice, KV caching is enabled by default in most decoder models. For instance, the Hugging Face Transformers library automatically caches past_key_values when use_cache=True, so each new decoding step concatenates the latest key/value with the previous cache instead of recomputing it. Below is a simplified PyTorch-style pseudocode illustrating this mechanism: ## Simplified KV cache usage in PyTorch if cache[\\\"key\\\"] is None: cache[\\\"key\\\"] = new_key # first token: initialize cache cache[\\\"\\n\\nQuantization of KV: AQUA-KV (2024) (\\u201cCache Me If You Must\\u201d) dynamically quantizes KV tensors to shrink memory while maintaining accuracy. By adaptively allocating precision based on content, AQUA-KV achieved higher compression rates on LLaMA 3.x models compared to static quantization, supporting extremely long contexts (aiming for 10M token inference). Other works like KVQuant pursue 4-bit or mixed-precision KV caching to reach context lengths that were previously infeasible. Sparse or Retrievable KV: Beyond compression, another direction is to avoid storing all KV by using sparse attention patterns. RetrievalAttention (2024) proposes offloading past keys/values to CPU and using approximate nearest-neighbor search to retrieve only the most relevant ones for each new token. It addresses distribution mismatches with an attention-aware retrieval algorithm, achieving near-full accuracy while accessing just 1\\u20133% of cached data.\\n\\nAvoiding Recomputing Attention: In a transformer decoder, generating each new token involves computing self-attention against all prior tokens. KV caching mitigates this by storing past keys and values so that each iteration only computes attention for the latest token\\u2019s queries. This yields a dramatic speedup: after the first token (which computes full attention for the prompt), subsequent tokens reuse cached KV pairs and only add one new key and value each time. The result is a roughly constant per-token latency after the first token, greatly improving throughput for long sequences. Challenges with Cache Growth: A drawback is that the KV cache grows linearly with sequence length, consuming substantial memory. For each generated token, caches must store a key and value vector per transformer layer and head. For example, LLaMA-2 13B uses ~1MB of cache per output token.\\n\\nMemory Management Strategies: Because naive caching can bloat memory, LLMs incorporate strategies to manage cache size: Sliding Window Cache: Some models (e.g. Mistral-7B) limit cache length by evicting old tokens beyond a window. Mistral uses a sliding window of W (4096 in some configs) where only the most recent W tokens\\u2019 KV pairs are kept; older ones are dropped as new tokens come in. Despite this, the model can still indirectly leverage older context through Transformer layers stacking (each layer\\u2019s state can carry forward information). Sliding Window Attention allows supporting long contexts (e.g. 16K) with a fixed memory cost instead of linear growth. Grouped-Query Attention (GQA): Reduces the number of KV vectors by using fewer \\u201ceffective\\u201d heads. For example, LLaMA-2 70B employs GQA to share key/value projections across groups of heads, cutting memory usage with minimal quality loss.\",\n  1476\t        \"is_citable\": true,\n  1477\t        \"prompt_context_metadata\": {\n  1478\t          \"age\": \"May 5, 2025\",\n  1479\t          \"url\": \"https://www.rohan-paul.com/p/kv-caching-in-llm-inference-a-comprehensive\",\n  1480\t          \"search_provider\": \"anthropic\"\n  1481\t        }\n  1482\t      }\n  1483\t    ],\n  1484\t    \"is_error\": false,\n  1485\t    \"structured_content\": null,\n  1486\t    \"meta\": null,\n  1487\t    \"message\": null,\n  1488\t    \"integration_name\": null,\n  1489\t    \"integration_icon_url\": null,\n  1490\t    \"display_content\": null\n  1491\t  },\n  1492\t  {\n  1493\t    \"start_timestamp\": \"2026-01-10T14:24:13.055846Z\",\n  1494\t    \"stop_timestamp\": \"2026-01-10T14:24:15.169806Z\",\n  1495\t    \"flags\": null,\n  1496\t    \"type\": \"thinking\",\n  1497\t    \"thinking\": \"\\u8fd9\\u4e9b\\u7ed3\\u679c\\u4e3b\\u8981\\u5173\\u4e8eKV cache\\u7684\\u6280\\u672f\\u7ec6\\u8282\\u3002\\u8ba9\\u6211\\u641c\\u7d22\\u66f4\\u6df1\\u5c42\\u7684\\u95ee\\u9898\\uff1aattention\\u673a\\u5236\\u4f5c\\u4e3a\\u4e00\\u79cd\\u68c0\\u7d22\\u673a\\u5236\\uff0c\\u4ee5\\u53ca\\u662f\\u5426\\u6709\\u4eba\\u5c1d\\u8bd5\\u5c06LLM\\u7684\\u8ba1\\u7b97\\u8fc7\\u7a0b\\u5916\\u5316\\u4e3a\\u5916\\u90e8\\u8bb0\\u5fc6\\u7cfb\\u7edf\\u3002\",\n  1498\t    \"summaries\": [],\n  1499\t    \"cut_off\": false,\n  1500\t    \"alternative_display_type\": null\n",

Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) have emerged as a promising tool for disentangling the complex, high-dimensional representations within LLMs into meaningful, interpretable features [9, 13, 7, 8]. However, recent work [29] suggests that even massively wide SAEs, trained on vast amounts of data, may only be scratching the surface in terms of capturing the full spectrum of concepts present in these \\u00b7 model representations. A significant portion of rare or highly specific concepts remain essentially \\u00b7 invisible due to their infrequent activation. These elusive features, akin to \\u201cdark matter\\u201d in the universe \\u00b7 of interpretability, pose a significant challenge for understanding and mitigating potential risks \\u00b7 associated with LLMs. While larger SAEs did exhibit some features for rarer concepts, the researchers \\u00b7\\n\\nfor automatically interpreting the features they learn. Existing works [4, 29] have employed LLMs \\u00b7 like GPT-4 to generate neuron explanations based on strongly activating text, or analyze SAE features. We employ a sequence-level classification task for interpretability evaluation [18]. Instead of pre- dicting feature activation at each token, we task an LLM with identifying whether entire sequences \\u00b7 contain a given feature. This simplified task requires fewer few-shot examples, fewer input/output \\u00b7 tokens, and allows for the use of smaller, faster LLMs while maintaining reliable scores. We utilize \\u00b7 Claude 3.5 Sonnet [2] as both the Interpreter and the Predictor in our automated interpretability \\u00b7 framework. The Interpreter generates explanations for each feature based on the top 10 activating \\u00b7 examples (see Appendix E for examples). Subsequently, the Predictor receives these explanations \\u00b7\\n\\nand retrieve relevant examples from the LLM\\u2019s pretraining corpus. To create an SSAE, we finetune \\u00b7 a pretrained general-purpose SAE (GSAE) on this curated subdomain data using Equation 1. The \\u00b7 GSAE is initially trained to reconstruct activations on a large, general-purpose dataset, enabling it to \\u00b7 capture a broad range of concepts. Finetuning on the subdomain data allows the SAE to specialize and \\u00b7 learn features that may be infrequent in the general domain but prevalent within the target subdomain. To evaluate the quality of the trained SAEs, we use two metrics: L0 and Perplexity with SAE [8]. After matching feature neurons across models via activation correlation, ...\\n\\nWe investigate feature universality in large language models (LLMs), a research field that aims to understand how different models similarly represent concepts in the latent spaces of their intermediate layers. Demonstrating feature universality allows discoveries about latent representations to generalize across several models. However, comparing features across LLMs is challenging due to polysemanticity, in which individual neurons often correspond to multiple features rather than distinct ones. This makes it difficult to disentangle and match features across different models. To address this issue, we employ a method known as dictionary learning by using sparse autoencoders (SAEs) to transform LLM activations into more interpretable spaces spanned by neurons corresponding to individual features. After matching feature neurons across models via activation correlation, we apply representational space similarity metrics like Singular Value Canonical Correlation Analysis to analyze thes\\n\\nComparing features across LLMs is inherently difficult due to the phenomenon of polysemanticity, where individual neurons often correspond to multiple features rather than distinct ones Elhage et al. (2022). This superposition of features makes it challenging to disentangle and match representations across different models. To address these challenges, we propose a novel approach leveraging sparse autoencoders (SAEs) to transform LLM activations into more interpretable spaces. SAEs allow us to decompose the complex, superposed representations within LLMs into distinct features that are easier to analyze and compare across models (Makhzani & Frey, 2013; Cunningham et al., 2023; Bricken et al., 2023). We then apply specific representational space similarity metrics to these SAE features, enabling a rigorous quantitative analysis of feature universality across different LLMs (Klabunde et al., 2023).\\n\\nSuperposition is a phenomenon in which a model, in response to the issue of having to represent more features than it has parameters, learns feature representations distributed across many parameters (Elhage et al., 2022). This causes its parameters, or neurons, to be polysemantic, which means that each neuron is involved in representing more than one feature. This leads to issues for interpretability, which aims to disentangle and cleanly identify features in models in order to manipulate them, such as by steering a model away from deception (Templeton et al., 2024). To address the issue of polysemanticity, Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) have been applied to disentangle an LLM\\u2019s polysemantic neuron activations into monosemantic \\\"feature neurons\\\", which are encouraged to represent an isolated concept (Makhzani & Frey, 2013; Cunningham et al., 2023; Gao et al., 2024; Rajamanoharan et al., 2024a; b).\\n\\nRepresentational Space Similarity. Previous work has studied neuron activation spaces by utilizing metrics to compare the geometric similarities of representational spaces (Raghu et al., 2017; Wang et al., 2018; Kornblith et al., 2019; Klabunde et al., 2024; 2023; Kriegeskorte et al., 2008). It was found that even models with different architectures may share similar representation spaces, hinting at feature universality. However, these techniques have yet to be applied to the feature spaces of sparse autoencoders trained on LLMs. Moreover, these techniques compare the similarity of paired input activations. Our work differs as it compares the similarity of paired feature weights. Essentially, previous works do not show that one can match LLMs by features that both LLMs find in common. Feature Manifolds. In the LLM era, bandwidth constraints drive architectural innovations like Mixture of Experts (MoE), Grouped Query Attention (GQA), Multi-Head Latent Attention (MLA), and State Space Models (SSM). MoE can be conceptualized as trained dynamic sparsity, with notable similarities to hard attention mechanisms when viewing expert weights as activated tokens. This layer deals with the resources needed by a model and the constraints of a pod - the minimum repeatable deployment unit. As an example, DeepSeek V3 has an official implementation with inference pods of 176 GPUs and a training pod (whole cluster) of 2048 GPUs. At the system level, we pay less attention to the internal details of a model, but abstract it as an elastic program (engine) with computation, memory, and communication need.\\n\\nFirst, any system is designed to achieve specific objectives within given constraints. For LLM systems, the most critical objectives are throughput and latency. These two objectives must be optimized within three fundamental constraints: compute (hardware operation speed and supported types), memory (capacity and hierarchy), and communication (memory & network bandwidth, latency, and hierarchy). Google provides excellent coverage of these concepts in the Roofline section of their Scaling Book. To navigate the complexity of modern LLM systems, I find it helpful to think in terms of three distinct abstraction layers. Each layer addresses different types of constraints and optimization opportunities, creating a good framework for understanding and improvement, which we will cover in details. The table below summarizes the basics of 3 abstraction layers.\\n\\nEarly examples include Group Convolution, designed to reduce convolution\\u2019s computational density. In the LLM era, bandwidth constraints drive architectural innovations like Mixture of Experts (MoE), Grouped Query Attention (GQA), Multi-Head Latent Attention (MLA), and State Space Models (SSM). MoE can be conceptualized as trained dynamic sparsity, with notable similarities to hard attention mechanisms when viewing expert weights as activated tokens. This layer deals with the resources needed by a model and the constraints of a pod - the minimum repeatable deployment unit. As an example, DeepSeek V3 has an official implementation with inference pods of 176 GPUs and a training pod (whole cluster) of 2048 GPUs. At the system level, we pay less attention to the internal details of a model, but abstract it as an elastic program (engine) with computation, memory, and communication need.\\n\\nWhile the 3-layer abstraction provides a useful framework for understanding most LLM systems, certain architectures and techniques transcend these boundaries. Dataflow Architectures and Megakernels. Some ASICs (e.g., SambaNova, Cerebras, Groq) employ data-centric programming models that execute operations immediately when partial data becomes available. In these programming models, a \\u201ckernel\\u201d can encompass an entire transformer block or even the complete model (see SambaNova\\u2019s HotChips 2024 presentation). This design provides significant latency advantages at the cost of programming complexity. The use of high-bandwidth on-chip SRAM is a different topic which is worth another article. Recent GPU community has explored similar concepts, such as MegaKernel, which aims to eliminate GPU idle timethrough massive kernel fusion. Speculative decoding.\\n\\nLet\\u2019s examine each layer in detail, starting from the lowest level of abstraction. The kernel layer focuses on software execution at the micro-architecture level. A kernel is the smallest unit of workload executed on an accelerator, including GPU. The programming model at this layer maps available hardware resources (general-purpose cores, matrix multiplication units, near-processor memory, GPU memory) to programming concepts (threads & blocks, local memory, global memory) and exposes the necessary instructions for users to manipulate them. CUDA C/C++ language has been the de facto standard for GPU kernel programming. As the need of kernel programming increases in recent years, a number of choices at this layer have become available. Two different trends are observed, which are not mutually exclusive: Tile languages like Triton, CuTile, and TileLang elevate the control granularity from thread to block and data granularity from scalar to tile.\",\n  2996\t        \"is_citable\": true,\n  2997\t        \"prompt_context_metadata\": {\n  2998\t          \"url\": \"https://ralphmao.github.io/ML-software-system/\",\n  2999\t          \"search_provider\": \"anthropic\"\n  3000\t        }\n",

[\n  2375\t      {\n  2376\t        \"type\": \"knowledge\",\n  2377\t        \"title\": \"Interpretability of LLMs: The Role of Sparse Autoencoders | Aqsone\",\n  2378\t        \"url\": \"https://www.aqsone.com/en/blog/interpretability-of-llms-the-role-of-sparse-autoencoders\",\n  2379\t        \"metadata\": {\n  2380\t          \"type\": \"webpage_metadata\",\n  2381\t          \"site_domain\": \"aqsone.com\",\n  2382\t          \"favicon_url\": \"https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?sz=64&domain=aqsone.com\",\n  2383\t          \"site_name\": \"Aqsone\"\n  2384\t        },\n  2385\t        \"is_missing\": false,\n  2386\t        \"text\": \"Encoding Intermediate Activations: ... (e.g., 12,288 dimensions for GPT-3). The SAE encodes this vector into a new, high-dimensional representation that is sparse (most values are zero)....\\n\\nTo understand how SAEs contribute to the interpretability of LLMs, we must first understand the concept of an autoencoder. An autoencoder is a neural network that learns to compress and decompress its input. Imagine a neural network that takes a 100-dimensional vector as input, reduces it to 50 dimensions, and then reconstructs it back to 100 dimensions. The goal of the model is to minimize the difference between the input vector and the reconstructed output. ... Sparse Autoencoders distinguish themselves by adding a sparsity penalty to their loss function, encouraging the model to activate only a small proportion of neurons in the intermediate layers. This sparsity penalty pushes the model to create intermediate vectors (activations) with as many zero values as possible. As we\\u2019ll see, this penalty enables the creation of compressed representations, making the intermediate activations of larger models like LLMs more interpretable.\\n\\nThe idea of using SAEs for the interpretability of LLMs revolves around decomposing their intermediate activations to make them more comprehensible to humans. The activations of a language model are often opaque: a single neuron can encode multiple concepts simultaneously, a phenomenon known as superposition. This complexity makes it challenging to discern what each neuron represents. SAEs can be trained on the intermediate activations of LLMs at various points in their architecture, often between two layers. Each component of these activations, extracted through the training of an SAE, is called a feature. A feature aims to represent an identifiable concept interpretable by humans (e.g., semantic concepts like \\\"human flaws\\\" or \\\"price increases,\\\" grammatical categories, or specific themes). In summary, the goal of using SAEs is to achieve a sparse representation (with as many zero values as possible) where each feature corresponds to an interpretable concept.\\n\\nEncoding Intermediate Activations: A SAE takes as input the intermediate activations of an LLM\\u2014these activations are often very large vectors (e.g., 12,288 dimensions for GPT-3). The SAE encodes this vector into a new, high-dimensional representation that is sparse (most values are zero). Sparsity and Concept Reduction: This encoding process enforces a sparsity constraint. Although the representation may have high dimensionality, only a small fraction of neurons are activated. For instance, in a 49,512-dimensional space, perhaps only 100 dimensions are nonzero. The goal is to decompose the activations into distinct components where each feature is distinct and easier to interpret. Feature Learning: During training, the SAE learns to identify combinations of neurons corresponding to important concepts in the LLM. The SAE\\u2019s decoder is then used to reconstruct the original activations from this compressed version.\\n\\nThis process, which identifies precise concepts from LLM activations, also enables direct control over the model\\u2019s behavior. Using a technique called feature steering, researchers can directly manipulate the activation of a feature to guide the model\\u2019s responses toward a specific topic. For instance, researchers at Anthropic showed that by artificially amplifying the activation of the feature associated with the Golden Gate Bridge, they could make Claude systematically reference the bridge in its responses, even when it was irrelevant. When asked to describe its physical form, Claude responded: \\u201cI am the Golden Gate Bridge... my physical form is the iconic bridge itself.\\u201d \\u00b7 Recently, significant efforts have been made by companies like OpenAI and Anthropic to develop methods for decomposing language models using Sparse Autoencoders. OpenAI succeeded in breaking down the activations of GPT-4 into 16 million potentially interpretable features.\",\n  2387\t        \"is_citable\": true,\n  2388\t        \"prompt_context_metadata\": {\n  2389\t          \"url\": \"https://www.aqsone.com/en/blog/interpretability-of-llms-the-role-of-sparse-autoencoders\",\n  2390\t          \"search_provider\": \"anthropic\"\n  2391\t        }\n  2392\t      },\n  2393\t      {\n  2394\t        \"type\": \"knowledge\",\n  2395\t        \"title\": \"Open the Artificial Brain: Sparse Autoencoders for LLM Inspection | Towards Data Science\",\n  2396\t        \"url\": \"https://towardsdatascience.com/open-the-artificial-brain-sparse-autoencoders-for-llm-inspection-c845f2a3f786/\",\n  2397\t        \"metadata\": {\n  2398\t          \"type\": \"webpage_metadata\",\n  2399\t          \"site_domain\": \"towardsdatascience.com\",\n  2400\t          \"favicon_url\": \"https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?sz=64&domain=towardsdatascience.com\",\n",

examine the representations within each layer. For example, I submitted texts like \\u201cAn apple\\u201d and extracted the residual stream activity. Then, I used the activations to fit a support vector machine (SVM) predicting whether the object (apple) contains a given property (e.g., is edible). The layers where the activations produce the most accurate classifier constitute the layers that most represent this item-level semantic property. I applied this general probing approach across several experiments, also studying two-item relations and four-item analogies \\u2013 aspects of semantic processing less often examined in interpretability research. ... Analyses of Llama-3.2-3b (28 layers) support the common abstraction-hierarchy perspective: In short texts, item-level semantics are most represented in early layers (layers 2-7), two-item relations in deeper layers (layers 8-12), and then four-item analogies deeper still (l\\n\\nMethods: I submitted very short texts to an LLM and used a probing approach to examine the representations within each layer. For example, I submitted texts like \\u201cAn apple\\u201d and extracted the residual stream activity. Then, I used the activations to fit a support vector machine (SVM) predicting whether the object (apple) contains a given property (e.g., is edible). The layers where the activations produce the most accurate classifier constitute the layers that most represent this item-level semantic property. I applied this general probing approach across several experiments, also studying two-item relations and four-item analogies \\u2013 aspects of semantic processing less often examined in interpretability research. ... Analyses of Llama-3.2-3b (28 layers) support the common abstraction-hierarchy perspective: In short texts, item-level semantics are most represented in early layers (layers 2-7), two-item relations in deeper layers (layers 8-12), and then four-item analogies deeper still (l\\n\\nSome interesting patterns emerge, although here I will just focus on what is relevant to understanding abstraction hierarchies: The representation of item-level semantics in the residual stream and feed-forward network output peaks in early layers (layers 2-7) before descending but turning upwards again in the final layers. This initial peak is consistent with the model steadily increasing in its abstraction, such that layers 2-7 are most sensitive to item-level semantics. The final rise has been demonstrated in some prior reports and presumably reflects the model preparing its actual word predictions. The main focus here, however, is on that initial peak and what it indicates regarding the level of targeted abstraction. I next examined how transformers represent the relation between two items. I did several experiments. The tests examined how an LLM encodes: (A) whether an object is likely to be found in a scene, (B) how semantically related humans report two objects to be, and (C) mo\\n\\nIf the peaks are taken to shed light on abstraction, this may reflect an increase of abstraction (producing the first peak then dip), then a decrease (producing the second peak), and then a further increase in abstraction (producing the second-half descent). None of the experiments on the smaller Llama-3.2-3b produced double peaks suggesting that this is an emergent property of scaling. Each of the results here replicates when tested using Qwen-2.5-72b but not Deepseek-V2.5-236b (the latter is a mixture-of-experts model). Figure 5. Buried information representation. Several but not all of the earlier experiments were performed again, now using Llama-3.3-70b-Instruct rather than Llama-3.2-3b. Each subfigure here maps to one of the subfigures from Figures 1, 2, 3, or 4. The mappings are specifically: a. Figure 1a, b. Figure 1b, c. Figure 2a left, d. Figure 2b, e. Figure 2c, f. Figure 3c, g. Figure 4c, h. Figure 4d, and i. Figure 4h. To better understand the dip-valley-dip representationa\\n\\nThis investigation reveals both expected hierarchical trends in LLM functioning along with deviations from these trends.[4] These findings hopefully refine prevailing assumptions about transformer dynamics and open avenues for exploring how architectural innovations and scaling influence the emergent properties of modern LLMs. The methodology employed here \\u2013 targeting very short texts and probing with classifiers \\u2013 does not seem to be a typical interpretability method. However, the multi-item relation and analogy topics studies here seem like they would be harder to study with more common techniques. The findings on buried concepts may have some importance for potential future activation steering work. Stimulating particularly deep layers may effectively simulate encountering a concept historically in the context window, which could be useful.\",\n  3033\t        \"is_citable\": true,\n  3034\t        \"prompt_context_metadata\": {\n  3035\t          \"age\": \"March 24, 2025\",\n  3036\t          \"url\": \"https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/c5jueEHxgLmYED6bj/hardcorellama_pcb_mar24_lw_post\",\n  3037\t          \"search_provider\": \"anthropic\"\n  3038\t        }\n  3039\t      },\n  3040\t      {\n  3041\t        \"type\": \"knowledge\",\n  3042\t        \"title\": \"Hyperdimensional Probe: Decoding LLM Representations via Vector Symbolic Architectures\",\n  3043\t        \"url\": \"https://arxiv.org/html/2509.25045\",\n  3044\t        \"metadata\": {\n  3045\t          \"type\": \"webpage_metadata\",\n  3046\t          \"site_domain\": \"arxiv.org\",\n  3047\t          \"favicon_url\": \"https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?sz=64&domain=arxiv.org\",\n  3048\t          \"site_name\": \"arXiv\"\n  3049\t        },\n  3050\t        \"is_missing\": false,\n",

When Claude needs to work with professional documents (.docx files) for: (1) Creating new documents, (2) Modifying or editing content, (3) Working with tracked changes, (4) Adding comments, or any other document tasks\"\n     4\tlicense: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms\n     5\t---\n     6\t\n     7\t# DOCX creation, editing, and analysis\n     8\t\n     9\t## Overview\n    10\t\n    11\tA .docx file is a ZIP archive containing XML files.\n    12\t\n    13\t## Quick Reference\n    14\t\n    15\t| Task | Approach |\n    16\t|------|----------|\n    17\t| Read/analyze content | `pandoc` or unpack for raw XML |\n    18\t| Create new document | Use `docx-js` - see Creating New Documents below |\n    19\t| Edit existing document | Unpack \u2192 edit XML \u2192 repack - see Editing Existing Documents below |\n    20\t\n    21\t### Reading Content\n    22\t\n    23\t```bash\n    24\t# Text extraction with tracked changes\n    25\tpandoc --track-changes=all document.docx -o output.md\n    26\t\n    27\t# Raw XML access\n    28\tpython scripts/unpack.py document.docx unpacked/\n    29\t```\n    30\t\n    31\t### Converting to Images\n    32\t\n    33\t```bash\n    34\tsoffice --headless --convert-to pdf document.docx\n    35\tpdftoppm -jpeg -r 150 document.pdf page\n    36\t```\n    37\t\n    38\t---\n    39\t\n    40\t## Creating New Documents\n    41\t\n    42\tGenerate .docx files with JavaScript. Install: `npm install -g docx`\n    43\t\n    44\t### Setup\n    45\t```javascript\n    46\tconst { Document, Packer, Paragraph, TextRun, Table, TableRow, TableCell, ImageRun,\n    47\t        Header, Footer, AlignmentType, PageOrientation, LevelFormat, ExternalHyperlink,\n    48\t        TableOfContents, HeadingLevel, BorderStyle, WidthType, ShadingType,\n    49\t        VerticalAlign, PageNumber, PageBreak } = require('docx');\n    50\t\n    51\tconst doc = new Document({ sections: [{ children: [/* content */] }] });\n    52\tPacker.toBuffer(doc).then(buffer => fs.writeFileSync(\"doc.docx\", buffer));\n    53\t```\n    54\t\n    55\t### Page Size\n    56\t\n    57\t```javascript\n    58\t// CRITICAL: docx-js defaults to A4, not US Letter\n    59\t// Always set page size explicitly for consistent results\n    60\tsections: [{\n    61\t  properties: {\n    62\t    page: {\n    63\t      size: {\n    64\t        width: 12240,   // 8.5 inches in DXA\n    65\t        height: 15840   // 11 inches in DXA\n    66\t      },\n    67\t      margin: { top: 1440, right: 1440, bottom: 1440, left: 1440 } // 1 inch margins\n    68\t    }\n    69\t  },\n    70\t  children: [/* content */]\n    71\t}]\n    72\t```\n    73\t\n    74\t**Common page sizes (DXA units, 1440 DXA = 1 inch):**\n    75\t\n    76\t| Paper | Width | Height | Content Width (1\" margins) |\n    77\t|-------|-------|--------|---------------------------|\n    78\t| US Letter | 12,240 | 15,840 | 9,360 |\n    79\t| A4 (default) | 11,906 | 16,838 | 9,026 |\n    80\t\n    81\t### Styles (Override Built-in Headings)\n    82\t\n    83\tUse Arial as the default font (universally supported). Keep titles black for readability.\n    84\t\n    85\t```javascript\n    86\tconst doc = new Document({\n    87\t  styles: {\n    88\t    default: { document: { run: { font: \"Arial\", size: 24 } } }, // 12pt default\n    89\t    paragraphStyles: [\n    90\t      // IMPORTANT: Use exact IDs to override built-in styles\n    91\t      { id: \"Heading1\", name: \"Heading 1\", basedOn: \"Normal\", next: \"Normal\", quickFormat: true,\n    92\t        run: { size: 32, bold: true, font: \"Arial\" },\n    93\t        paragraph: { spacing: { before: 240, after: 240 }, outlineLevel: 0 } }, // outlineLevel required for TOC\n    94\t      { id: \"Heading2\", name: \"Heading 2\", basedOn: \"Normal\", next: \"Normal\", quickFormat: true,\n    95\t        run: { size: 28, bold: true, font: \"Arial\" },\n    96\t        paragraph: { spacing: { before: 180, after: 180 }, outlineLevel: 1 } },\n    97\t    ]\n    98\t  },\n    99\t  sections: [{\n   100\t    children: [\n   101\t      new Paragraph({ heading: HeadingLevel.HEADING_1, children: [new TextRun(\"Title\")] }),\n   102\t    ]\n   103\t  }]\n   104\t});\n   105\t```\n   106\t\n   107\t### Lists (NEVER use unicode bullets)\n   108\t\n   109\t```javascript\n   110\t// \u274c WRONG - never manually insert bullet characters\n   111\tnew Paragraph({ children: [new TextRun(\"\u2022 Item\")] })  // BAD\n   112\tnew Paragraph({ children: [new TextRun(\"\\u2022 Item\")] })  // BAD\n   113\t\n   114\t// \u2705 CORRECT - use numbering config with LevelFormat.BULLET\n   115\tconst doc = new Document({\n   116\t  numbering: {\n   117\t    config: [\n   118\t      { reference: \"bullets\",\n   119\t        levels: [{ level: 0, format: LevelFormat.BULLET, text: \"\u2022\", alignment: AlignmentType.LEFT,\n   120\t          style: { paragraph: { indent: { left: 720, hanging: 360 } } } }] },\n   121\t      { reference: \"numbers\",\n   122\t        levels: [{ level: 0, format: LevelFormat.DECIMAL, text: \"%1.\", alignment: AlignmentType.LEFT,\n   123\t          style: { paragraph: { indent: { left: 720, hanging: 360 } } } }] },\n   124\t    ]\n   125\t  },\n   126\t  sections: [{\n   127\t    children: [\n   128\t      new Paragraph({ numbering: { reference: \"bullets\", level: 0 },\n   129\t        children: [new TextRun(\"Bullet item\")] }),\n   130\t      new Paragraph({ numbering: { reference: \"numbers\", level: 0 },\n   131\t        children: [new TextRun(\"Numbered item\")] }),\n   132\t    ]\n   133\t  }]\n   134\t});\n   135\t\n   136\t// \u26a0\ufe0f Each reference creates INDEPENDENT numbering\n   137\t// Same reference = continues (1,2,3 then 4,5,6)\n   138\t// Different reference = restarts (1,2,3 then 1,2,3)\n   139\t```\n   140\t\n   141\t### Tables\n   142\t\n   143\t**CRITICAL: Tables need dual widths** - set both `columnWidths` on the table AND `width` on each cell. Without both, tables render incorrectly on some platforms.\n   144\t\n   145\t```javascript\n   146\t// CRITICAL: Always set table width for consistent rendering\n   147\t// CRITICAL: Use ShadingType.CLEAR (not SOLID) to prevent black backgrounds\n   148\tconst border = { style: BorderStyle.SINGLE, size: 1, color: \"CCCCCC\" };\n   149\tconst borders = { top: border, bottom: border, left: border, right: border };\n   150\t\n   151\tnew Table({\n   152\t  width: { size: 100, type: WidthType.PERCENTAGE }, // Always set table width\n   153\t  columnWidths: [4680, 4680], // Set at table level (DXA: 1440 = 1 inch)\n   154\t  rows: [\n   155\t    new TableRow({\n   156\t      children: [\n   157\t        new TableCell({\n   158\t          borders,\n   159\t          width: { size: 4680, type: WidthType.DXA }, // Also set on each cell\n   160\t          shading: { fill: \"D5E8F0\", type: ShadingType.CLEAR }, // CLEAR not SOLID\n   161\t          margins: { top: 80, bottom: 80, left: 120, right: 120 }, // Cell padding (internal, not added to width)\n   162\t          children: [new Paragraph({ children: [new TextRun(\"Cell\")] })]\n   163\t        })\n   164\t      ]\n   165\t    })\n   166\t  ]\n   167\t})\n   168\t```\n   169\t\n   170\t**Table width calculation:**\n   171\t\n   172\tUse `WidthType.PERCENTAGE` for simplicity, or `WidthType.DXA` for precise control:\n   173\t\n   174\t```javascript\n   175\t// Option 1: Percentage (recommended - automatically fits content area)\n   176\twidth: { size: 100, type: WidthType.PERCENTAGE }\n   177\t\n   178\t// Option 2: DXA (precise control)\n   179\t// Table width = sum of columnWidths = content width\n   180\t// US Letter with 1\" margins: 12240 - 2880 = 9360 DXA\n   181\twidth: { size: 9360, type: WidthType.DXA },\n   182\tcolumnWidths: [7000, 2360]  // Must sum to table width\n   183\t```\n   184\t\n   185\t**Width rules:**\n   186\t- Table width must equal the sum of `columnWidths`\n   187\t- Cell `width` must match corresponding `columnWidth`\n   188\t- Cell `margins` are internal padding - they reduce content area, not add to cell width\n   189\t- For full-width tables: use content width (page width minus left and right margins)\n   190\t\n   191\t### Images\n   192\t\n   193\t```javascript\n   194\t// CRITICAL: type parameter is REQUIRED\n   195\tnew Paragraph({\n   196\t  children: [new ImageRun({\n   197\t    type: \"png\", // Required: png, jpg, jpeg, gif, bmp, svg\n   198\t    data: fs.readFileSync(\"image.png\"),\n   199\t    transformation: { width: 200, height: 150 },\n   200\t    altText: { title: \"Title\", description: \"Desc\", name: \"Name\" } // All three required\n   201\t  })]\n   202\t})\n   203\t```\n   204\t\n   205\t### Page Breaks\n   206\t\n   207\t```javascript\n   208\t// CRITICAL: PageBreak must be inside a Paragraph\n   209\tnew Paragraph({ children: [new PageBreak()] })\n   210\t\n   211\t// Or use pageBreakBefore\n   212\tnew Paragraph({ pageBreakBefore: true, children: [new TextRun(\"New page\")] })\n   213\t```\n   214\t\n   215\t### Table of Contents\n   216\t\n   217\t```javascript\n   218\t// CRITICAL: Headings must use HeadingLevel ONLY - no custom styles\n   219\tnew TableOfContents(\"Table of Contents\", { hyperlink: true, headingStyleRange: \"1-3\" })\n   220\t```\n   221\t\n   222\t### Headers/Footers\n   223\t\n   224\t```javascript\n   225\tsections: [{\n   226\t  properties: {\n   227\t    page: { margin: { top: 1440, right: 1440, bottom: 1440, left: 1440 } } // 1440 = 1 inch\n   228\t  },\n   229\t  headers: {\n   230\t    default: new Header({ children: [new Paragraph({ children: [new TextRun(\"Header\")] })] })\n   231\t  },\n   232\t  footers: {\n   233\t    default: new Footer({ children: [new Paragraph({\n   234\t      children: [new TextRun(\"Page \"), new TextRun({ children: [PageNumber.CURRENT] })]\n   235\t    })] })\n   236\t  },\n   237\t  children: [/* content */]\n   238\t}]\n   239\t```\n   240\t\n   241\t### Critical Rules for docx-js\n   242\t\n   243\t- **Set page size explicitly** - docx-js defaults to A4; use US Letter (12240 x 15840 DXA) for US documents\n   244\t- **Never use `\\n`** - use separate Paragraph elements\n   245\t- **Never use unicode bullets** - use `LevelFormat.BULLET` with numbering config\n   246\t- **PageBreak must be in Paragraph** - standalone creates invalid XML\n   247\t- **ImageRun requires `type`** - always specify png/jpg/etc\n   248\t- **Always set table `width`** - use `{ size: 100, type: WidthType.PERCENTAGE }` for full width\n   249\t- **Tables need dual widths** - `columnWidths` array AND cell `width`, both must match\n   250\t- **Table width = sum of columnWidths** - for DXA, ensure they add up exactly\n   251\t- **Always add cell margins** - use `margins: { top: 80, bottom: 80, left: 120, right: 120 }` for readable padding\n   252\t- **Use `ShadingType.CLEAR`** - never SOLID for table shading\n   253\t- **TOC requires HeadingLevel only** - no custom styles on heading paragraphs\n   254\t- **Override built-in styles** - use exact IDs: \"Heading1\", \"Heading2\", etc.\n   255\t- **Include `outlineLevel`** - required for TOC (0 for H1, 1 for H2, etc.)\n   256\t\n   257\t---\n   258\t\n   259\t## Editing Existing Documents\n   260\t\n   261\t**Follow all 3 steps in order.**\n   262\t\n   263\t### Step 1: Unpack\n   264\t```bash\n   265\tpython scripts/unpack.py document.docx unpacked/\n   266\t```\n   267\tExtracts XML, pretty-prints, merges adjacent runs, and converts smart quotes to XML entities (`&#x201C;` etc.) so they survive editing. Use `--merge-runs false` to skip run merging.\n   268\t\n   269\t### Step 2: Edit XML\n   270\t\n   271\tEdit files in `unpacked/word/`. See XML Reference below for patterns.\n   272\t\n   273\t**Always use \"Claude\" as the author** for tracked changes and comments.\n   274\t\n   275\t**Use the Edit tool directly for string replacement. Do not write Python scripts.** Scripts introduce unnecessary complexity. The Edit tool shows exactly what is being replaced.\n   276\t\n   277\t**CRITICAL: Use smart quotes for new content.** When adding text with apostrophes or quotes, use XML entities to produce smart quotes:\n   278\t```xml\n   279\t<!-- Use these entities for professional typography -->\n   280\t<w:t>Here&#x2019;s a quote: &#x201C;Hello&#x201D;</w:t>\n   281\t```\n   282\t| Entity | Character |\n   283\t|--------|-----------|\n   284\t| `&#x2018;` | \u2018 (left single) |\n   285\t| `&#x2019;` | \u2019 (right single / apostrophe) |\n   286\t| `&#x201C;` | \u201c (left double) |\n   287\t| `&#x201D;` | \u201d (right double) |\n   288\t\n   289\t**Adding comments:** Use `comment.py` to handle boilerplate across multiple XML files (text must be pre-escaped XML):\n   290\t```bash\n   291\tpython scripts/comment.py unpacked/ 0 \"Comment text with &amp; and &#x2019;\"\n   292\tpython scripts/comment.py unpacked/ 1 \"Reply text\" --parent 0  # reply to comment 0\n   293\t```\n   294\tThen add markers to document.xml (see Comments in XML Reference).\n   295\t\n   296\t### Step 3: Pack\n   297\t```bash\n   298\tpython scripts/pack.py unpacked/ output.docx --original document.docx\n   299\t```\n   300\tValidates with auto-repair, condenses XML, and creates DOCX. Use `--validate false` to skip.\n   301\t\n   302\t**Auto-repair will fix:**\n   303\t- `durableId` >= 0x7FFFFFFF (regenerates valid ID)\n   304\t- Missing `xml:space=\"preserve\"` on `<w:t>` with whitespace\n   305\t\n   306\t**Auto-repair won't fix:**\n   307\t- Malformed XML, invalid element nesting, missing relationships, schema violations\n   308\t\n   309\t### Common Pitfalls\n   310\t\n   311\t- **Replace entire `<w:r>` elements**: When adding tracked changes, replace the whole `<w:r>...</w:r>` block with `<w:del>...<w:ins>...` as siblings. Don't inject tracked change tags inside a run.\n   312\t- **Preserve `<w:rPr>` formatting**: Copy the original run's `<w:rPr>` block into your tracked change runs to maintain bold, font size, etc.\n   313\t\n   314\t---\n   315\t\n   316\t## XML Reference\n   317\t\n   318\t### Schema Compliance\n   319\t\n   320\t- **Element order in `<w:pPr>`**: `<w:pStyle>`, `<w:numPr>`, `<w:spacing>`, `<w:ind>`, `<w:jc>`, `<w:rPr>` last\n   321\t- **Whitespace**: Add `xml:space=\"preserve\"` to `<w:t>` with leading/trailing spaces\n   322\t- **RSIDs**: Must be 8-digit hex (e.g., `00AB1234`)\n   323\t\n   324\t### Tracked Changes\n   325\t\n   326\t**Insertion:**\n   327\t```xml\n   328\t<w:ins w:id=\"1\" w:author=\"Claude\" w:date=\"2025-01-01T00:00:00Z\">\n   329\t  <w:r><w:t>inserted text</w:t></w:r>\n   330\t</w:ins>\n   331\t```\n   332\t\n   333\t**Deletion:**\n   334\t```xml\n   335\t<w:del w:id=\"2\" w:author=\"Claude\" w:date=\"2025-01-01T00:00:00Z\">\n   336\t  <w:r><w:delText>deleted text</w:delText></w:r>\n   337\t</w:del>\n   338\t```\n   339\t\n   340\t**Minimal edits** - only mark what changes:\n   341\t```xml\n   342\t<!-- Change \"30 days\" to \"60 days\" -->\n   343\t<w:r><w:t>The term is </w:t></w:r>\n   344\t<w:del w:id=\"1\" w:author=\"Claude\" w:date=\"...\">\n   345\t  <w:r><w:delText>30</w:delText></w:r>\n   346\t</w:del>\n   347\t<w:ins w:id=\"2\" w:author=\"Claude\" w:date=\"...\">\n   348\t  <w:r><w:t>60</w:t></w:r>\n   349\t</w:ins>\n   350\t<w:r><w:t> days.</w:t></w:r>\n   351\t```\n   352\t\n   353\t**Rejecting another author's insertion** - nest deletion inside their insertion:\n   354\t```xml\n   355\t<w:ins w:author=\"Jane\" w:id=\"5\">\n   356\t  <w:del w:author=\"Claude\" w:id=\"10\">\n   357\t    <w:r><w:delText>their inserted text</w:delText></w:r>\n   358\t  </w:del>\n   359\t</w:ins>\n   360\t```\n   361\t\n   362\t**Restoring another author's deletion** - add insertion after (don't modify their deletion):\n   363\t```xml\n   364\t<w:del w:author=\"Jane\" w:id=\"5\">\n   365\t  <w:r><w:delText>deleted text</w:delText></w:r>\n   366\t</w:del>\n   367\t<w:ins w:author=\"Claude\" w:id=\"10\">\n   368\t  <w:r><w:t>deleted text</w:t></w:r>\n   369\t</w:ins>\n   370\t```\n   371\t\n   372\t### Comments\n   373\t\n   374\tAfter running `comment.py` (see Step 2), add markers to document.xml. For replies, use `--parent` flag and nest markers inside the parent's:\n   375\t\n   376\t```xml\n   377\t<!-- Comment 0 with reply 1 nested inside -->\n   378\t<w:commentRangeStart w:id=\"0\"/>\n   379\t  <w:commentRangeStart w:id=\"1\"/>\n   380\t  <w:r><w:t>text</w:t></w:r>\n   381\t  <w:commentRangeEnd w:id=\"1\"/>\n   382\t<w:commentRangeEnd w:id=\"0\"/>\n   383\t<w:r><w:rPr><w:rStyle w:val=\"CommentReference\"/></w:rPr><w:commentReference w:id=\"0\"/></w:r>\n   384\t<w:r><w:rPr><w:rStyle w:val=\"CommentReference\"/></w:rPr><w:commentReference w:id=\"1\"/></w:r>\n   385\t```\n   386\t\n   387\t### Images\n   388\t\n   389\t1. Add image file to `word/media/`\n   390\t2. Claude\u56de\u7b54\n\n\u8fd9\u4e2a\u95ee\u9898\u6781\u5176\u6df1\u523b\u3002\u4f60\u5b9e\u9645\u4e0a\u5728\u95ee\uff1a**LLM\u5904\u7406\u4e0a\u4e0b\u6587\u7684\u8ba1\u7b97\u673a\u5236\u672c\u8eab\uff0c\u80fd\u5426\u88ab\"\u5916\u5316\"\u6210\u4e00\u79cd\u8bb0\u5fc6\u67b6\u6784\uff1f**\n\n\u8ba9\u6211\u6df1\u5165\u641c\u7d22\u8fd9\u4e2a\u65b9\u5411\u2014\u2014\u8fd9\u89e6\u53ca\u4e86LLM\u53ef\u89e3\u91ca\u6027\u3001Memory-Augmented \u672c\u8d28\u4e0a\u5c31\u662f\"\u53ef\u5fae\u5206\u7684\u8f6f\u68c0\u7d22\"\n\n\u4f60\u7684\u76f4\u89c9\u662f\u5bf9\u7684\u3002Transformer\u7684attention\u673a\u5236\uff0c\u4ece\u8ba1\u7b97\u539f\u7406\u4e0a\u770b\uff1a\n\n```\nAttention(Q, K, V) = softmax(QK^T / \u221ad) \u00b7 V\n```\n\n\u8fd9\u4e2a\u516c\u5f0f\u7684\u8bed\u4e49\u662f\uff1a\n- **Q (Query)**\uff1a\u6211\u5728\"\u95ee\u4ec0\u4e48\u95ee\u9898\"\n- **K (Key)**\uff1a\u6bcf\u4e2a\u8bb0\u5fc6\u5355\u5143\u7684\"\u7d22\u5f15/\u6807\u7b7e\"\n- **V (Value)**\uff1a\u6bcf\u4e2a\u8bb0\u5fc6\u5355\u5143\u7684\"\u5b9e\u9645\u5185\u5bb9\"\n- **softmax(QK^T)**\uff1a\u76f8\u4f3c\u5ea6\u52a0\u6743 \u2192 \u672c\u8d28\u662f**\u8f6f\u6027\u68c0\u7d22**\n\n**Attention\u5c31\u662f\u4e00\u4e2a\u53ef\u5fae\u5206\u7684\u3001\u8f6f\u6027\u7684\u8bb0\u5fc6\u68c0\u7d22\u7cfb\u7edf\u3002**\n\n---\n\n### \u5b66\u672f\u754c\u786e\u5b9e\u5728\u6cbf\u7740\u4f60\u7684\u601d\u8def\u8d70\n\n| \u7814\u7a76 | \u6838\u5fc3\u601d\u60f3 | \u4e0e\u4f60\u8bbe\u60f3\u7684\u5173\u7cfb |\n|-----|---------|--------------|\n| **Memorizing Transformers** (ICLR 2022) | \u5c06KV pairs\u5b58\u5165\u5916\u90e8kNN\u6570\u636e\u5e93\uff0c\u7528attention query\u68c0\u7d22 | \u53d1\u73b0\u4e86\u4e13\u95e8\u8d1f\u8d23\"\u68c0\u7d22\"\u7684attention \u4f60\u8bbe\u60f3\u7684\"\u667a\u80fd\u5916\u8111\"\u67b6\u6784\n\n\u6839\u636e\u4f60\u7684\u63cf\u8ff0\uff0c\u4f60\u60f3\u8981\u7684\u662f\uff1a\n\n```\n\u6587\u672c\u5757 \u2192 LLM\u4f5c\u4e3a\"\u667a\u80fd\u77e5\u8bc6\u56fe\u8c31\" \u2192 \n    \u251c\u2500\u2500 \u63d0\u53d6\u663e\u5f0f\u6982\u5ff5\n    \u251c\u2500\u2500 \u63a8\u65ad\u9690\u5f0f\u6982\u5ff5  \n    \u251c\u2500\u2500 \u8ba1\u7b97\u6982\u5ff5-\u6587\u672c\u8ddd\u79bb\n    \u2514\u2500\u2500 \u6784\u5efa\u5b9a\u5236\u5316\u8bed\u4e49\u5b50\u56fe\n        \u2193\n    \u7528\u4e8e\u667a\u80fd\u68c0\u7d22\uff08\u6a21\u4effLLM\u5185\u90e8\u7684attention\u68c0\u7d22\u673a\u5236\uff09\n```\n\n**\u8fd9\u4e2a\u67b6\u6784\u7684\u5173\u952einsight\u662f**\uff1a\n\n1. **LLM\u672c\u8eab\u5c31\u662f\u4e00\u4e2a\"\u667a\u80fd\u7684\u8bb0\u5fc6\u68c0\u7d22\u7cfb\u7edf\"**\u2014\u2014\u901a\u8fc7\u8bad\u7ec3\u5b66\u4f1a\u4e86\u4ec0\u4e48\u4fe1\u606f\u4e0e\u4ec0\u4e48\u67e5\u8be2\u76f8\u5173\n2. **KV Cache\u672c\u8d28\u662f\"\u5de5\u4f5c\u8bb0\u5fc6\"**\u2014\u2014\u4f46\u53d7\u9650\u4e8e\u4e0a\u4e0b\u6587\u7a97\u53e3\n3. \u56de\u5230\u4f60\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\u95ee\u9898\n\n> **\"\u8bb0\u5fc6\u7684\u673a\u5236\uff0c\u6216\u8bb8\u5c31\u85cf\u5728LLM\u539f\u7406\u672c\u8eab\u5f53\u4e2d\"**\n\n\u4f60\u662f\u5bf9\u7684\u3002\u5b66\u672f\u754c\u7684\u53d1\u73b0\u652f\u6301\u8fd9\u4e2a\u89c2\u70b9\uff1a\n\n1. **Attention = \u53ef\u5fae\u5206\u68c0\u7d22**\uff1aQK\u76f8\u4f3c\u5ea6\u8ba1\u7b97\u672c\u8d28\u662f\u8bb0\u5fc6\u5730\u5740\u67e5\u627e\n2. **KV Cache = **\u538b\u7f29\u8bb0\u5fc6**\uff1a\u957f\u671f\u4fe1\u606f\u53ef\u4ee5\u88ab\u538b\u7f29\u5b58\u50a8\uff0c\u6309\u9700\u68c0\u7d22\n\n**\u4f46\u7f3a\u5931\u7684\u4e00\u73af\u662f**\uff1a\u5982\u4f55\u8ba9\u5916\u90e8\u8bb0\u5fc6\u5177\u6709\u4e0eLLM\u5185\u90e8\u8868\u793a**\u76f8\u540c\u7684\u8bed\u4e49\u7a7a\u95f4**\uff1f\n\n\u8fd9\u6b63\u662f **KEPLER**\uff08\u6e05\u534e\uff09\u7b49\u5de5\u4f5c\u8bd5\u56fe\u89e3\u51b3\u7684\u2014\u2014\u5c06\u6587\u672c\u548c\u77e5\u8bc6\u56fe\u8c31\u5b9e\u4f53\u5d4c\u5165\u5230**\u540c\u4e00\u4e2a\u5411\u91cf\u7a7a\u95f4**\uff0c\u4f7f\u5f97\"\u5916\u8111\"\u548c\"\u5185\u8111\"\u8bf4\u540c\u4e00\u79cd\u8bed\u8a00\u3002\n\n---\n\n# \u7528\u6237\u63d0\u95ee\u4e8c\uff1a\u7528LLM\u6fc0\u6d3b\u6a21\u5f0f\u4f5c\u4e3a\u8bed\u4e49\u5b50\u56fe\n\n## \u7528\u6237\u539f\u6587\n\n> \u6587\u672c\u5757 \u2192 Rate\u3002\n\n## Claude\u56de\u7b54\n\n\u8fd9\u4e2a\u60f3\u6cd5\u975e\u5e38\u7cbe\u5f69\uff01\u4f60\u5b9e\u9645\u4e0a\u5728\u8bf4\uff1a\n\n**\u4e0d\u8981\u8ba9LLM\"\u7ffb\u8bd1\"\u6210\u4eba\u7c7b\u53ef\u8bfb\u7684\u8bed\u4e49\u56fe\u8c31\uff0c\u800c\u662f\u76f4\u63a5\u7528LLM\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3b\u6a21\u5f0f\u672c\u8eab\u4f5c\u4e3a\u8bed\u4e49\u8868\u793a\u3002**\n\n\u8fd9\u662f\u4e00\u4e2a\u6839\u672c\u6027\u7684\u89c6\u89d2\u8f6c\u6362\u3002\u8ba9\u6211\u641c\u7d22\u662f\u5426\u6709\u4eba\u5728\u505a\u7c7b\u4f3c\u7684\u4e8b\u60c5\u3002\n\n---\n\n### Interpret and Control Dense Retrieval with SAE (2025.2)\n\n\u5c06sparse autoencoders\u5e94\u7528\u4e8edense embeddings\uff0c\u52a0\u5165\u9762\u5411\u68c0\u7d22\u7684recovery loss\u786e\u4fdd\u63d0\u53d6\u7684\u7a00\u758f\u7279\u5f81\u5bf9\u68c0\u7d22\u4fdd\u6301\u4fdd\u771f\u5ea6\u3002\u4f7f\u7528\u5b66\u5230\u7684\u7a00\u758f\u7279\u5f81\u8fdb\u884c\u68c0\u7d22\uff0c\u4ee5\u53ca\u7528\u91cd\u5efa\u7684embeddings\u8fdb\u884c\u68c0\u7d22\uff0c\u90fd\u80fd\u6062\u590d\u539f\u59cbdense retrieval\u5728MsMarco\u548cBeir\u57fa\u51c6\u4e0a\u7684\u5927\u90e8\u5206\u51c6\u786e\u7387\u3002\n\n**\u6838\u5fc3\u53d1\u73b0**\uff1a\u7528SAE\u7684\u7a00\u758f\u6fc0\u6d3b\u76f4\u63a5\u505a\u68c0\u7d22\uff0cMRR\u8fbe\u52300.3306\uff08\u539f\u59cb0.3605\uff09\uff0c**\u7279\u5f81\u672c\u8eab\u5c31\u662f\u68c0\u7d22\u7684\u6709\u6548\u8868\u793a**\u3002\n\n#### 2. Towards Data-Centric Interpretability with SAEs (LessWrong, 2025)\n\n\u7528sparse autoencoders\u8fdb\u884c\u56db\u79cd\u6587\u672c\u6570\u636e\u5206\u6790\u4efb\u52a1\u2014\u2014\u6570\u636e\u5bf9\u6bd4\u3001\u76f8\u5173\u6027\u53d1\u73b0\u3001\u5b9a\u5411\u805a\u7c7b\u548c**\u68c0\u7d22**\u3002SAE\u6355\u83b7\u7684\u4e0d\u4ec5\u4ec5\u662f\u8bed\u4e49\u4fe1\u606f\uff0c\u5b83\u4eec\u903c\u8fd1LLM\u7684\u8868\u793a\uff0c\u7f16\u7801\u4e86\u590d\u6742\u7684\u8bed\u8a00\u548c\u6982\u5ff5\u4fe1\u606f\u3002SAE\u53ef\u4ee5\u7528\u4e8e**\u57fa\u4e8e\u5c5e\u6027\u7684\u67e5\u8be2\u68c0\u7d22\u6570\u636e**\u3002\n\n#### 3. Steering Semantic Search with SAE Features (NeurIPS 2024 Workshop)\n\n\u5c06SAEs\u5e94\u7528\u4e8e\u5927\u8bed\u8a00\u6a21\u578b\u7684dense text embeddings\uff0c\u8bc1\u660e\u5b83\u4eec\u80fd\u6709\u6548\u5730\u89e3\u7ea0\u7f20\u6587\u6863\u7ea7\u8bed\u4e49\u6982\u5ff5\u3002\u901a\u8fc7\u572842\u4e07\u7bc7\u79d1\u5b66\u8bba\u6587\u6458\u8981\u7684embeddings\u4e0a\u8bad\u7ec3SAE\uff0c\u7a00\u758f\u8868\u793a\u80fd\u5728\u4fdd\u6301\u8bed\u4e49\u4fdd\u771f\u5ea6\u7684\u540c\u65f6\u63d0\u4f9b\u9ad8\u5ea6\u53ef\u89e3\u91ca\u6027\u3002\n\n---\n\n### \u4f60\u7684\u601d\u8def\u6bd4\u5b66\u672f\u754c\u66f4\u6fc0\u8fdb\u7684\u5730\u65b9\n\n\u5b66\u672f\u754c\u76ee\u524d\u7684\u505a\u6cd5\u662f\uff1a\n\n```\n\u6fc0\u6d3b \u2192 SAE \u2192 \u76f4\u63a5\u7528**\u539f\u59cb\u6fc0\u6d3b** |\n| \u76ee\u6807\uff1a\u7406\u89e3+\u68c0\u7d22 | \u76ee\u6807\uff1a**\u6700\u5f3a\u68c0\u7d22** |\n\n---\n\n### \u4f60\u7684\u60f3\u6cd5\u7684\u6570\u5b66\u8868\u8ff0\n\n\u8bbe $\\mathcal{M}$ \u662f\u4e00\u4e2aLLM\uff0c$d$ \u662f\u4e00\u4e2a\u6587\u6863\u5757\u3002\n\n**\u4f20\u7edfembedding**\uff1a\n$$\\text{repr}(d) = \\text{Embed}(d) \\in \\mathbb{R}^{768}$$\n\n**\u4f60\u7684\u60f3\u6cd5**\uff1a\n$$\\text{repr}(d, \\mathcal{M}) = \\{\\text{activation}_l(d, \\mathcal{M})\\}_{l=1}^{L} \\in \\mathbb{R}^{L \\times H \\times D}$$\n\n\u5176\u4e2d $L$ \u662f\u5c42\u6570\uff0c$H$ \u662fattention heads\uff0c$D$ \u662fhidden dimension\u3002\n\n**\u68c0\u7d22**\uff1a\n1. \u5bf9query $q$\uff0c\u5148\u505aHyDE\u751f\u6210\u5047\u8bbe\u6587\u6863 $\\hat{d} = \\text{HyDE}(q)$\n2. \u8ba1\u7b97 $\\text{repr}(\\hat{d}, \\mathcal{M})$\n3. \u627e\u5230\u8ddd\u79bb\u6700\u8fd1\u7684\u6587\u6863\uff1a$\\arg\\min_d \\text{dist}(\\text{repr}(\\hat{d}, \\mathcal{M}), \\text{repr}(d, \\mathcal{M}))$\n\n**\u5173\u952e\u6d1e\u89c1**\uff1a\u8fd9\u4e2a\u8868\u793a\u662f **specific to $(d, \\mathcal{M})$** \u7684\uff0c\u6362\u4e2aLLM\u5c31\u9700\u8981\u91cd\u65b0\u8ba1\u7b97\u3002\u4f46\u8fd9\u4fdd\u8bc1\u4e86**\u5bf9\u4e8e\u8fd9\u4e2aLLM\u6765\u8bf4\uff0c\u662f\u6700\u4f18\u7684\u8bed\u4e49\u8868\u793a**\u3002\n\n---\n\n### \u5b9e\u73b0\u8def\u5f84\n\n```python\n# \u4f2a\u4ee3\u7801\uff1a\u57fa\u4e8e\u6fc0\u6d3b\u7684\u68c0\u7d22\u7cfb\u7edf\n\nclass ActivationFingerprint:\n    def __init__(self, model, layers_to_capture=[12, 16, 20, 24]):\n        self.model = model\n        self.layers = layers_to_capture\n        self.hooks = []\n        self.activations = {}\n        \n    def capture(self, text):\n        \"\"\"\u6355\u83b7\u6587\u672c\u5728\u6307\u5b9a\u5c42\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3b\"\"\"\n        # \u6ce8\u518cforward hooks\n        for layer_idx in self.layers:\n            hook = self.model.layers[layer_idx].register_forward_hook(\n                lambda m, i, o: self.activations[layer_idx] = o\n            )\n            self.hooks.append(hook)\n        \n        # \u524d\u5411\u4f20\u64ad\n        self.model(text)\n        \n        # \u8fd4\u56de\u6fc0\u6d3b\"\u6307\u7eb9\"\n        fingerprint = self._aggregate_activations()\n        return fingerprint\n    \n    def _aggregate_activations(self):\n        \"\"\"\u805a\u5408\u591a\u5c42\u6fc0\u6d3b\u4e3a\u5355\u4e00\u8868\u793a\"\"\"\n        # \u65b9\u68481: \u62fc\u63a5 + \u964d\u7ef4\n        # \u65b9\u68482: \u53ea\u7528\u6700\u540e\u4e00\u5c42\u7684mean pooling\n        # \u65b9\u68483: \u7528SAE\u7a00\u758f\u5316\n        return sparse_encode(concat(self.activations.values()))\n\nclass ActivationRetriever:\n    def __init__(self, fingerprinter, index):\n        self.fp = fingerprinter\n        self.index = index  # FAISS / Milvus\n        \n    def index_document(self, doc_id, text):\n        fingerprint = self.fp.capture(text)\n        self.index.add(doc_id, fingerprint)\n        \n    def search(self, query, top_k=10):\n        # HyDE: \u751f\u6210\u5047\u8bbe\u6587\u6863\n        hyde_doc = generate_hypothetical_doc(query)\n        \n        # \u83b7\u53d6\u67e5\u8be2\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3b\u6307\u7eb9\n        query_fp = self.fp.capture(hyde_doc)\n        \n        # \u68c0\u7d22\n        return self.index.search(query_fp, top_k)\n```\n\n---\n\n### \u8fd9\u4e2a\u65b9\u5411\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\u4f18\u52bf\n\n1. **\u6700\u5927\u5316\u5229\u7528LLM\u7684\u5185\u90e8\u77e5\u8bc6**\uff1a\u4e0d\u662f\u8ba9LLM\"\u8bf4\u51fa\"\u5b83\u77e5\u9053\u4ec0\u4e48\uff0c\u800c\u662f\u76f4\u63a5\u7528\u5b83\"\u60f3\"\u7684\u8fc7\u7a0b\n\n2. **\u5929\u7136\u89e3\u51b3\u9690\u5f0f\u6982\u5ff5\u95ee\u9898**\uff1aLLM\u5728\u5904\u7406\u6587\u672c\u65f6\uff0c\u5185\u90e8\u6fc0\u6d3b\u5df2\u7ecf\u5305\u542b\u4e86\u6240\u6709\u5b83\u80fd\u63a8\u65ad\u51fa\u7684\u9690\u5f0f\u6982\u5ff5\n\n3. **\u907f\u514d\"\u7ffb\u8bd1\u635f\u8017\"**\uff1a\u4f20\u7edf\u65b9\u6cd5\u8ba9LLM\u751f\u6210\u5173\u952e\u8bcd/\u6458\u8981\uff0c\u518d\u7528\u8fd9\u4e9b\u6587\u672c\u505a\u68c0\u7d22\u3002\u4f60\u7684\u65b9\u6cd5\u8df3\u8fc7\u4e86\u8fd9\u4e2a\u635f\u8017\n\n4. **\u6700\u5f3a\u7684\u53ec\u56de\u4fdd\u8bc1**\uff1a\u5982\u679c\u4e00\u4e2a\u67e5\u8be2\u80fd\u8ba9LLM\"\u60f3\u5230\"\u67d0\u4e2a\u6587\u6863\uff0c\u90a3\u5b83\u4eec\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3b\u6a21\u5f0f\u5fc5\u7136\u76f8\u4f3c\n\n---\n\n# \u7528\u6237\u63d0\u95ee\u4e09\uff1a\u6fc0\u6d3b\u6307\u7eb9\u65b9\u6cd5\u7684\u5c40\u9650\u6027\n\n## \u7528\u6237\u539f\u6587\n\n> \u8fd9\u4e2a\u65b9\u6cd5\u6709\u5b83\u7684\u5c40\u9650\u6027\uff0c\u4e8b\u5b9e\u4e0a\uff0c\u5bf9\u4e8e\u8bed\u4e49\u589e\u5e7f\u6765\u8bf4\uff0c\u76f8\u5f53\u4e8e\u6fc0\u6d3b\u4e86LLM\u66f4\u591a\u7684\u8282\u70b9\u3002\u65e0\u8bba\u662f\u539f\u6587\u672c\u5757\uff0c\u8fd8\u662fHyDE\u8fc7\u7684\u95ee\u9898\uff0c\u90fd\u662f\u76f8\u5f53\u4e8e\u5728\u6fc0\u6d3b\u66f4\u591a\u7684LLM\u8282\u70b9\uff0c\u4ece\u800c\u8ba9\u672c\u6765\u76f4\u63a5\u7ecf\u8fc7LLM\u7684\u4e24\u4e2a\u6587\u672c\uff0c\u5b83\u4eec\u7684\"\u6fc0\u6d3b\u77e9\u9635\"\u53ef\u80fd\u662f\u5927\u76f8\u5f84\u5ead\u7684\uff0c\u4ece\u800c\u5b8c\u5168\u9519\u8fc7\u4e86\u5f7c\u6b64\uff0c\u6bd4\u5982\u5b83\u4eec\u90fd\u7528\u4e86\u53cd\u8bc1\u6cd5\uff0c\u4f46\u662f\u8c01\u4e5f\u6ca1\u6709\u63d0\u8fc7\u53cd\u8bc1\u6cd5\u8fd9\u4e09\u4e2a\u5b57\uff0c\u8fd9\u6837\u8fd9\u4e24\u4e2a\u8bc1\u660e\u5c31\u5b8c\u5168\u6709\u53ef\u80fd\u5f7c\u6b64\u9519\u8fc7\u2014\u2014\u5b83\u4eec\u5b8c\u5168\u53ef\u80fd\u5728LLM\u7684\u5185\u90e8\u795e\u7ecf\u8282\u70b9\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3b\u884c\u4e3a\u4e0a\uff0c\u5b8c\u5168\u4e0d\u540c\u3002\u4f46\u662f\u8bed\u4e49\u589e\u5e7f\uff0c\u6fc0\u6d3b\u4e86\u66f4\u591a\u7684LLM\u8282\u70b9\uff0c\u5c24\u5176\u662f\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\uff0c\u4e5f\u5c31\u662f\u66f4\u4e3a\u62bd\u8c61\u7684\u8282\u70b9\u3002\u4ece\u800c\u7ed9\u8fd9\u4e24\u4e2a\u8bc1\u660e\u8fc7\u7a0b\u76f4\u63a5\u642d\u5efa\u4e86\u6865\u6881\u3002\u6240\u4ee5\u6211\u521a\u521a\u8bf4\u7684\u8fd9\u79cd\u65b9\u6cd5\uff0c\u5176\u5b9e\u53ef\u80fd\u5e76\u4e0d\u662f\u80fd\u591f\u8d85\u8d8a\u8bed\u4e49\u589e\u5e7f\u6280\u672f\u7684\u65b9\u6cd5\u3002\n\n## \u8fd9\u63ed\u793a\u4e86\u4e00\u4e2a\u66f4\u6df1\u7684\u95ee\u9898\n\nLLM\u5728\u5904\u7406\u6587\u672c\u65f6\uff0c**\u4e0d\u4e00\u5b9a\u4f1a\"\u60f3\u5230\"\u6240\u6709\u76f8\u5173\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5**\u2014\u2014\u5b83\u53ea\u662f\u5728\u505anext prediction\u3002\n\n\u53ea\u6709\u5f53\u4f60**\u663e\u5f0f\u5730\u95ee\u5b83**\uff1a\"\u8fd9\u6bb5\u6587\u672c\u7528\u4e86\u4ec0\u4e48\u65b9\u6cd5\uff1f\"\u5b83\u624d\u4f1a\u6fc0\u6d3b\"\u53cd\u8bc1\u6cd5\"\u8fd9\u4e2a\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u3002\n\n\u6240\u4ee5\uff1a\n\n| \u90a3\u771f\u6b63\u7684\u89e3\u51b3\u65b9\u6848\u662f\u4ec0\u4e48\uff1f\n\n\u4f60\u7684\u5206\u6790\u6307\u5411\u4e00\u4e2a\u66f4\u7cbe\u5999\u7684\u67b6\u6784\uff1a\n\n```\n\u6587\u672c\u5757 \u2192 LLM\u5904\u7406 \u2192 \u540c\u65f6\u6355\u83b7\uff1a\n    \u2502\n    \u251c\u2500\u2500 \u4f4e\u9636\u6fc0\u6d3b\u6307\u7eb9\uff08\u5177\u4f53\u5185\u5bb9\uff09\n    \u2502\n    \u2514\u2500\u2500 \"\u8fd9\u6bb5\u6587\u672c\u7684\u65b9\u6cd5\u8bba\u662f\u4ec0\u4e48\uff1f\"\n       \"\u8fd9\u6bb5\u6587\u672c\u9690\u542b\u7684\u4e3b\u9898\u662f\u4ec0\u4e48\uff1f\"\n \u91cd\u65b0\u7406\u89e3\u8bed\u4e49\u589e\u5e7f\n\n\u4f20\u7edf\u89c2\u70b9\uff1a\n> \u8bed\u4e49\u589e\u5e7f = \u8ba9LLM\u751f\u6210\u989d\u5916\u7684\u5173\u952e\u8bcd/\u63cf\u8ff0 = \u4fe1\u606f\u589e\u76ca\n\n\u4f60\u7684\u89c2\u70b9\uff08\u66f4\u6df1\u523b\uff09\uff1a\n> \u8bed\u4e49\u589e\u5e7f = \u901a\u8fc7\u63d0\u95ee\uff0c\u5f3a\u5236LLM\u6fc0\u6d3b\u5176\u5185\u90e8\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u62bd\u8c61\u8282\u70b9 = **\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u7684\u663e\u5f0f\u5316**\n\n\u8fd9\u610f\u5473\u7740\u8bed\u4e49\u589e\u5e7f\u7684\u771f\u6b63\u4ef7\u503c\u4e0d\u5728\u4e8e\"\u751f\u6210\u7684\u6587\u5b57\"\uff0c\u800c\u5728\u4e8e**\u5b83\u89e6\u53d1\u4e86LLM\u5185\u90e8\u4ec0\u4e48\u6837\u7684\u8ba1\u7b97\u8fc7\u7a0b**\u3002\n\n---\n\n### \u4e00\u4e2a\u53ef\u80fd\u7684\u7edf\u4e00\u67b6\u6784\n\n```\n              \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n              \u2502         \u6587\u672c\u5757 d                \u2502\n              \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u252c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n                              \u2502\n                              \u25bc\n \u4f60\u7684\u6279\u8bc4\u7684\u672c\u8d28\n\n\u4f60\u5b9e\u9645\u4e0a\u5728\u8bf4\uff1a\n\n**\u5355\u7eaf\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3b\u6307\u7eb9\u662f\"\u88ab\u52a8\"\u7684\u2014\u2014\u5b83\u53ea\u6355\u83b7\u6587\u672c\u76f4\u63a5\u89e6\u53d1\u7684\u8282\u70b9\u3002**\n\n**\u8bed\u4e49\u589e\u5e7f\u662f\"\u4e3b\u52a8\"\u7684\u2014\u2014\u5b83\u901a\u8fc7\u63d0\u95ee\uff0c\u5f3a\u5236LLM\u53bb\u63a2\u7d22\u548c\u6fc0\u6d3b\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u3002**\n\n\u8fd9\u4e2a\u533a\u5206\u662f\u6839\u672c\u6027\u7684\u3002\u88ab\u52a8\u6355\u83b7\u4f1a\u9519\u8fc7\u9690\u5f0f\u8fde\u63a5\uff0c\u4e3b\u52a8\u63a2\u9488\u624d\u80fd\u53d1\u73b0\u6df1\u5c42\u7ed3\u6784\u3002\n\n\u6240\u4ee5\u6700\u4f18\u65b9\u6848\u53ef\u80fd\u662f\uff1a**\u88ab\u52a8\u6fc0\u6d3b + \u4e3b\u52a8\u63a2\u9488**\u7684\u7ed3\u5408\u3002\n\n---\n\n# \u7528\u6237\u63d0\u95ee\u56db\uff1a\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\u7684\"\u95ed\u5305\"\u4e0e\u7ed3\u6784\u6027\u6fc0\u6d3b\n\n## \u7528\u6237\u539f\u6587\n\n> \u6240\u4ee5\u6211\u4eec\u6cbf\u7740\u8fd9\u4e2a\u601d\u8def\u601d\u8003\uff0c\u5982\u679c\u6211\u4eec\u80fd\u591f\u6709\u4e00\u79cd\u65b9\u6cd5\uff0c\u4e0d\u662f\u901a\u8fc7\u8bed\u8a00\uff0c\u800c\u662f\u901a\u8fc7\u6570\u5b57\u7684\u5e8f\u5217\u2014\u2014\u5176\u5b9e\u4e5f\u5c31\u662f\u54ea\u4e9b\u4e2a\u7528\u6237\u63d0\u51fa\u7684\u95ee\u9898\uff0c\u6216\u8005\u522b\u7684\u4ec0\u4e48\u624b\u6bb5\uff0c\u80fd\u591f\u6709\u6548\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3bLLM\u5185\u90e8\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u3002\u5bf9\u4e8e\u4e00\u4e2a\u6587\u672c\u5757\uff0c\u6211\u4eec\u6216\u8bb8\u4f1a\u5f97\u5230\u4e00\u4e2a\u5b83\u5728LLM\u601d\u7ef4\u7a7a\u95f4\u4e2d\u7684\u4e00\u4e2a\u5927\u6982\u7684\u4f4d\u7f6e\u7684\u95ed\u5305\u3002\u8fd9\u4e2a\u95ed\u5305\u7684\u4e0b\u9762\u662f\u4f4e\u9636\u8282\u70b9\uff0c\u8fd9\u4e2a\u95ed\u5305\u7684\u4e0a\u9762\uff0c\u662f\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u3002\u800c\u5bf9\u4e8e\u6240\u6709\u53ef\u80fd\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\uff0c\u6211\u4eec\u6216\u8bb8\u80fd\u591f\u8ba1\u7b97\u51fa\u5b83\u4eec\u548c\u95ed\u5305\u4e4b\u95f4\u7684\u8ddd\u79bb\u3002\u751a\u81f3\uff0c\u6211\u4eec\u6709\u6280\u672f\u4e0aLLM\u81ea\u5df1\u8bf4\u51fa\u8fd9\u4e2a\u95ed\u5305\u2014\u2014\u4e5f\u5c31\u662f\u8fd9\u6bb5\u6587\u5b57\uff0c\u5728\u591a\u5927\u7a0b\u5ea6\u4e0a\u662f\u548c\u67d0\u4e2a\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5A\u76f8\u5173\u3002\u5176\u5b9e\u6211\u60f3\u8bf4\u7684\u662f\uff0c\u95ee\u9898\u5728\u4e8e\uff0c\u4e3a\u4ec0\u4e48\u4e00\u4e2a\u6587\u672c\u5757\uff0c\u672c\u6765\u6ca1\u6709\u6fc0\u6d3b\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\uff0c\u4f46\u662f\u5e26\u4e0a\u4e00\u4e2a\u7528\u6237\u7684\u95ee\u9898\uff0c\u5c31\u53ef\u4ee5\u6fc0\u6d3b\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u4e86\u5462\uff1f\u4f1a\u4e0d\u4f1a\u662f\u56e0\u4e3a\u67d0\u79cd\"\u8ddd\u79bb\"\u7684\u56e0\u7d20\u5462\uff1f\u6216\u8bb8\u5b9e\u9645\u53d1\u751f\u7684\u6545\u4e8b\u4e0d\u662f\u5173\u4e8e\u8ddd\u79bb\u7684\uff0c\u800c\u662f\u5173\u4e8e\u67d0\u79cd\u7ed3\u6784\u6027\u7684\u5b58\u5728\u2014\u2014\u5728LLM\u7684\u8ba1\u7b97\u4 Claude\u56de\u7b54\n\n\u4f60\u89e6\u53ca\u4e86\u4e00\u4e2a\u6781\u5176\u6df1\u523b\u7684\u95ee\u9898\u3002\u8ba9\u6211\u641c\u7d22\u4e00\u4e0b\u662f\u5426\u6709\u4eba\u5728\u7814\u7a76\u8fd9\u79cd\"\u7ed3\u6784\u6027\u6fc0\u6d3b\"\u7684\u673a\u5236\u3002\n\n---\n\n### \u5173\u952e\u53d1\u73b0\uff1aLLM\u5185\u90e8\u5b58\u5728\u660e\u786e\u7684\u5c42\u7ea7\u62bd\u8c61\u7ed3\u6784\n\nTransformer\u5c42\u9010\u6b65\u79ef\u7d2f\u4fe1\u606f\uff1a\u8bcd\u6c47\u7279\u5f81\u6700\u5148\u51fa\u73b0\uff0c\u7136\u540e\u662f\u8bed\u6cd5\uff0c\u63a5\u7740\u662f\u4e0a\u4e0b\u6587\u542b\u4e49\uff0c\u6700\u7ec8\u662f\u9ad8\u9636\u63a8\u7406\u3002\n\n\u5728Llama-3.2-3b (28\u5c42)\u4e2d\uff1a\n- \u8bcd\u6c47\u7ea7\u8bed\u4e49\u5728\u6d45\u5c42(2-7\u5c42)\u8868\u793a\u6700\u5f3a\n- \u4e24\u9879\u5173\u7cfb\u5728\u66f4\u6df1\u5c42(8-12\u5c42)\n- \u56db\u9879\u7c7b\u6bd4\u5728\u66f4\u6df1\u5c42\n\n\u8fd9\u8bc1\u5b9e\u4e86\u4f60\u7684\u76f4\u89c9\uff1a**\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u786e\u5b9e\u5b58\u5728\u4e8e\u6df1\u5c42**\u3002\n\n---\n\n### \u4f60\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\u95ee\u9898\uff1a\u4e3a\u4ec0\u4e48\u95ee\u9898\u80fd\"\u6fc0\u6d3b\"\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\uff1f\n\n\u8ba9\u6211\u753b\u51fa\u8fd9\u4e2a\u7ed3\u6784\uff1a\n\n```\n                    \u6df1\u5c42 (\u63a8\u7406/\u7c7b\u6bd4)\n                         \u25b2\n                         \u2502 \u2190 \u95ee\u9898\u7684Query\u5728\u8fd9\u91cc\"\u62c9\"\n                         \u2502\n                    \u4e2d\u5c42 (\u5173\u7cfb)\n                         \u25b2\n                         \u2502\n                    \u6d45\u5c42 (\u8bcd\u6c47)\n                         \u25b2\n                         \u2502\n                    [\u6587\u672c\u5757\u8f93\u5165]\n```\n\n**\u6ca1\u6709\u95ee\u9898\u65f6**\uff1a\u4fe1\u606f\"\u5411\u4e0a\"\u6d41\u52a8\uff0c\u4f46\u5728\u67d0\u4e2a\u5c42\u7ea7\u5c31\"\u5e73\u7a33\"\u4e86\u2014\u2014\u6ca1\u6709\u4ec0\u4e48\u5728\"\u62c9\"\u5b83\u7ee7\u7eed\u5411\u4e0a\n\n**\u6709\u95ee\u9898\u65f6**\uff1a\u95ee\u9898\u4f5c\u4e3aQuery\uff0c\u5728\u6df1\u5c42\"\u62c9\"\u6587\u672c\u5757\u7684\u4fe1\u606f\uff0c\u5f3a\u5236\u5339\u914d\u53d1\u751f\u5728\u9ad8\u9636\u5c42\n\n---\n\n### Attention\u7684\u672c\u8d28\u63ed\u793a\u4e86\u7b54\u6848\n\nAttention\u7684\u6570\u5b66\u516c\u5f0f\uff1a\n```\nAttention(Q, K, V) = softmax(QK^T / \u221ad) \u00b7 V\n```\n\n**\u5173\u952e\u6d1e\u5bdf**\uff1a\n- **\u6ca1\u6709Query\uff0c\u5c31\u6ca1\u6709\u68c0\u7d22**\n- \u6587\u672c\u5757\u5355\u72ec\u5904\u7406\u65f6\uff0c\u5b83\u53ea\u662f\u5728\u5404\u5c42\"\u5b58\u50a8\"\u4fe1\u606f\uff08\u751f\u6210K\u548cV\uff09\n- \u4f46\u6ca1\u6709\u4e1c\u897f\u5728\"\u95ee\"\u5b83\uff08\u6ca1\u6709Q\u6765\u5339\u914dK\uff09\n- \u6240\u4ee5\u9ad8\u9636\u4fe1\u606f**\u5b58\u5728**\u4f46**\u672a\u88ab\u63d0\u53d6**\n\n\u5f53\u95ee\u9898\u51fa\u73b0\u65f6\uff1a\n- \u95ee\u9898\u751f\u6210Query\n- Query\u5728\u5404\u5c42\u4e0e\u6587\u672c\u5757\u7684Key\u5339\u914d\n- **\u8fd9\u4e2a\u5339\u914d\u8fc7\u7a0b\u672c\u8eab\u5c31\u662f\"\u6fc0\u6d3b\"**\n\n---\n\n### \u4f60\u7684\u6df1\u523b\u6d1e\u5bdf\uff1a\u8fd9\u662f\u7ed3\u6784\u6027\u7684\uff0c\u4e0d\u662f\u5076\u7136\u7684\n\n\u7814\u7a76\u53d1\u73b0\uff1a\u4e00\u4e2asubvalue\u4e0d\u4ec5\u4f5c\u4e3a\"value\"\u76f4\u63a5\u8d21\u732e\u5206\u5e03\u53d8\u5316\uff0c\u8fd8\u4f5c\u4e3a\"query\"\u53bb\u6fc0\u6d3b\u4e0a\u5c42\u7684\u5176\u4ed6subvalues\u3002\u6bcf\u4e2a\u4e4b\u524d\u7684\u5c42\u90fd\u53c2\u4e0e\u8ba1\u7b97subvalue\u7684\u7cfb\u6570\u5f97\u5206\u3002\n\n\u8fd9\u610f\u5473\u7740\uff1a**\u8de8\u5c42\u6fc0\u6d3b\u662fTransformer\u7684\u5185\u5728\u7ed3\u6784**\uff0c\u4e0d\u662f\u5076\u7136\u73b0\u8c61\u3002\n\n---\n\n### \u90a3\u80fd\u5426\"\u4e0d\u901a\u8fc7\u8bed\u8a00\"\u76f4\u63a5\u6fc0\u6d3b\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\uff1f\n\n\u4f60\u7684\u8bbe\u60f3\u53ef\u4ee5\u8fd9\u6837\u7406\u89e3\uff1a\n\n```\n\u4f20\u7edf\u65b9\u6cd5\uff1a\n\u6587\u672c\u5757 \u2192 \u8bed\u8a00\u95ee\u9898 \u2192 Query\u5411\u91cf \u2192 \u6fc0\u6d3b\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\n\n\u4f60\u7684\u8bbe\u60f3\uff1a\n\u6587\u672c\u5757 \u2192 ???\uff08\u67d0\u79cd\u7ed3\u6784\u6027\u63a2\u9488\uff09\u2192 \u76f4\u63a5\u627e\u5230\"\u5e94\u8be5\u88ab\u6fc0\u6d3b\"\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\n```\n\n**\u53ef\u80fd\u7684\u65b9\u5411**\uff1a\n\n#### \u65b9\u54111\uff1a\u5c42\u7ea7\u63a2\u9488\uff08Hierarchical Probing\uff09\n\n\u4e3a\u6bcf\u4e2a\u62bd\u8c61\u5c42\u7ea7\u8bad\u7ec3\u4e00\u4e2a\u63a2\u9488\uff0c\u7cfb\u7edf\u6027\u5730\u63d0\u53d6\u6587\u672c\u5757\u5728\u5404\u5c42\u7684\u8868\u793a\uff1a\n\n```python\n# \u4f2a\u4ee3\u7801\ndef extract_hierarchical_fingerprint(text, model):\n    fingerprint = {}\n    for layer_idx in range(model.num_layers):\n        activation = model.get_activation(text, layer_idx)\n        fingerprint[layer_idx] = probe[layer_idx].extract_features(activation)\n    return fingerprint\n```\n\n#### \u65b9\u54112\uff1a\u865a\u62dfQuery\u6ce8\u5165\n\n\u4e0d\u7528\u8bed\u8a00\u751f\u6210Query\uff0c\u800c\u662f\u76f4\u63a5\u5728\u9ad8\u9636\u5c42\u6ce8\u5165\"\u63a2\u7d22\u6027Query\"\uff1a\n\n```python\ndef probe_high_order_concepts(text, model, probe_queries):\n    \"\"\"\n    probe_queries: \u4e00\u7ec4\u9884\u8bad\u7ec3\u7684\u5411\u91cf\uff0c\u4ee3\u8868\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\u7684\u57fa\n    \"\"\"\n    text_kv = model.get_kv(text)  # \u83b7\u53d6\u6587\u672c\u5757\u7684K,V\n    \n    activations = []\n    for probe_q in probe_queries:  # \u7528\u63a2\u9488Query\u53bb\"\u8be2\u95ee\"\n        attn = softmax(probe_q @ text_kv.K.T) @ text_kv.V\n        activations.append(attn)\n    \n    return activations  # \u8fd9\u5c31\u662f\u6587\u672c\u5757\u5728\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\u7684\u8868\u793a\n```\n\n#### \u65b9\u54113\uff1a\u8de8\u5c42\u68af\u5ea6\u8ffd\u8e2a\n\n\u5982\u679c\u6211\u4eec\u80fd\u8ffd\u8e2a\"\u4f4e\u9636\u8282\u70b9 \u2192 \u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\"\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3b\u8def\u5f84\uff0c\u5c31\u80fd**\u9884\u6d4b**\u54ea\u4e9b\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\"\u5e94\u8be5\"\u88ab\u6fc0\u6d3b\u3002\n\n---\n\n### \u4f60\u7684\u8bbe\u60f3\u7684\u672c\u8d28\n\n\u4f60\u5b9e\u9645\u4e0a\u5728\u8bf4\uff1a\n\n> **\u4e0d\u8981\u7528\"\u8bed\u8a00\"\u8fd9\u4e2a\u4e2d\u4ecb\u53bb\u6fc0\u6d3b\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u3002**\n> **\u76f4\u63a5\u5728\u795e\u7ecf\u7f51\u7edc\u7684\u8ba1\u7b97\u7ed3\u6784\u4e2d\uff0c\u627e\u5230\"\u6587\u672c\u5757 \u2192 \u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\"\u7684\u6fc0\u6d3b\u8def\u5f84\u3002**\n> **\u7136\u540e\uff0c\u7528\u540c\u6837\u7684\u65b9\u6cd5\u5904\u7406HyDE\u540e\u7684\u95ee\u9898\u3002**\n> **\u5982\u679c\u4e24\u8005\u6fc0\u6d3b\u4e86\u76f8\u540c\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\uff0c\u5c31\u8bf4\u660e\u5339\u914d\u3002**\n\n\u8fd9\u4e2a\u601d\u8def\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\u4f18\u52bf\u662f\uff1a\n\n| \u4f20\u7edf\u65b9\u6cd5 | \u4f60\u7684\u65b9\u6cd5 |\n|---------|---------|\n| \u6587\u672c\u5757 \u2192 LLM\u751f\u6210\u8bed\u4e49\u6807\u7b7e \u2192 \u5339\u914d | \u6587\u672c\u5757 \u2192 \u76f4\u63a5\u8ba1\u7b97\u9ad8\u9636\u6fc0\u6d3b \u2192 \u5339\u914d |\n| \u4f9d\u8d56LLM\"\u8bf4\u51fa\"\u6982\u5ff5 | \u76f4\u63a5\u5728\u6fc0\u6d3b\u7a7a\u95f4\u64cd\u4f5c |\n| \u6709\u7ffb\u8bd1\u635f\u8017 | \u65e0\u7ffb\u8bd1\u635f\u8017 |\n| \u9700\u8981\u8bbe\u8ba1prompt | \u53ef\u4ee5\u7cfb\u7edf\u5316/\u81ea\u52a8\u5316 |\n\n---\n\n### \u4f46\u8fd9\u91cc\u6709\u4e00\u4e2a\u5173\u952e\u95ee\u9898\n\n**Attention\u7684\u7ed3\u6784\u51b3\u5b9a\u4e86\uff1a\u6ca1\u6709Query\uff0c\u9ad8\u9636\u8282\u70b9\u5c31\u4e0d\u4f1a\u88ab\"\u6fc0\u6d3b\"**\n\n\u8fd9\u4e0d\u662f\u8ddd\u79bb\u95ee\u9898\uff0c\u800c\u662f**\u8ba1\u7b97\u8def\u5f84**\u95ee\u9898\uff1a\n- \u4fe1\u606f\u53ea\u6709\u5728\u88ab\"\u8be2\u95ee\"\u65f6\u624d\u4f1a\u5411\u4e0a\u6d41\u52a8\n- \u6ca1\u6709Query\uff0c\u4fe1\u606f\u5c31\"\u6c89\u7761\"\u5728\u5404\u5c42\n\n\u6240\u4ee5\u4f60\u7684\u65b9\u5411\u53ef\u80fd\u9700\u8981\uff1a\n\n1. **\u6784\u9020\u4e00\u7ec4\"\u901a\u7528\u63a2\u9488Query\"**\uff0c\u8986\u76d6\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\n2. **\u7528\u8fd9\u7ec4\u63a2\u9488\u7cfb\u7edf\u6027\u5730\"\u8be2\u95ee\"\u6bcf\u4e2a\u6587\u672c\u5757**\n3. **\u8bb0\u5f55\u6bcf\u4e2a\u63a2\u9488\u6fc0\u6d3b\u7684\u54cd\u5e94**\n4. **\u8fd9\u7ec4\u54cd\u5e94\u5c31\u662f\u6587\u672c\u5757\u7684\"\u9ad8\u9636\u6307\u7eb9\"**\n\n\u8fd9\u6837\uff0c\u4f60\u5c31\u4e0d\u9700\u8981\u7528\u81ea\u7136\u8bed\u8a00\u63d0\u95ee\uff0c\u800c\u662f\u7528\u4e00\u7ec4**\u5411\u91cf\u63a2\u9488**\u76f4\u63a5\u5728\u795e\u7ecf\u7f51\u7edc\u5185\u90e8\u64cd\u4f5c\u3002\n\n---\n\n# \u7528\u6237\u63d0\u95ee\u4e94\uff1a\u542f\u53d1\u5f0f\u63d0\u95ee\u8986\u76d6\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\n\n## \u7528\u6237\u539f\u6587\n\n> \u81ea\u9002\u5e94\u63a2\u6d4bPrompt\n\n## \u7b2c\u4e8c\u6b65\uff1a\u8fb9\u754c\u63a2\u6d4b\n\u5bf9\u4e8e\u4f60\u5217\u51fa\u7684\u6bcf\u4e2a\u6982\u5ff5C\uff0c\u8bf7\u56de\u7b54\uff1a\n- \u7b2c\u56db\u6b65\uff1a\u8fed\u4ee3\n\u5bf9\u7b2c\u4e09\u6b65\u4fdd\u7559\u7684\u65b0\u6982\u5ff5\uff0c\u91cd\u590d\u7b2c\u4e8c\u6b65\uff0c\u76f4\u5230\u6ca1\u6709\u65b0\u7684\u8fd1\u8ddd\u79bb\u6982\u5ff5\u51fa\u73b0\u3002\n```\n\n---\n\n### \u4e00\u4e2a\u5b8c\u6574\u7684\u63a2\u6d4b\u7b97\u6cd5\n\n```python\ndef explore_concept_space(text, llm, max_iterations=5, distance_threshold=4):\n    \"\"\"\n    \u542f\u53d1\u5f0f\u63a2\u6d4b\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\n    \"\"\"\n    discovered_concepts = set()\n    frontier = []  # \u5f85\u63a2\u7d22\u7684\u6982\u5ff5\n    \n    # \u7b2c\u4e00\u6b65\uff1a\u521d\u59cb\u626b\u63cf\n    initial_concepts = llm.ask(f\"\"\"\n        \u9605\u8bfb\u4ee5\u4e0b\u6587\u672c\uff0c\u5217\u51fa3-5\u4e2a\u6700\u6838\u5fc3\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u6807\u7b7e\uff1a\n        {text}\n    \"\"\")\n    frontier.extend(initial_concepts)\n    \n    for iteration in range(max_iterations):\n        if not frontier:\n            break\n            \n        current_concept = frontier.pop(0)\n        \n        if current_concept in discovered_concepts:\n            continue\n            \n        # \u63a2\u6d4b\u90bb\u5c45\u6982\u5ff5\n        neighbors = llm.ask(f\"\"\"\n            \u5bf9\u4e8e\u6982\u5ff5 [{current_concept}]\uff0c\u5728\u6587\u672c [{text}] \u7684\u8bed\u5883\u4e0b\uff1a\n            1. \u5217\u51fa\u5b83\u7684\u7236\u6982\u5ff5\u3001\u5144\u5f1f\u6982\u5ff5\u3001\u5b50\u6982\u5ff5\n            2. \u8bc4\u4f30\u6bcf\u4e2a\u6982\u5ff5\u4e0e\u539f\u6587\u672c\u7684\u8bed\u4e49\u8ddd\u79bb(1-10)\n            3. \u53ea\u8fd4\u56de\u8ddd\u79bb\u2264{distance_threshold}\u7684\u6982\u5ff5\n        \"\"\")\n        \n        discovered_concepts.add(current_concept)\n        \n        # \u5c06\u65b0\u53d1\u73b0\u7684\u8fd1\u8ddd\u79bb\u6982\u5ff5\u52a0\u5165frontier\n        for neighbor in neighbors:\n            if neighbor not in discovered_concepts:\n                frontier.append(neighbor)\n    \n    return discovered_concepts\n```\n\n---\n\n### \u66f4\u9ad8\u6548\u7684\u7b56\u7565\uff1a\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\u7684\"\u5750\u6807\u7cfb\"\n\n\u4e0e\u5176\u7528\u6811\u5f62\u63a2\u7d22\uff0c\u4e0d\u5982\u5efa\u7acb\u4e00\u4e2a**\u6982\u5ff5\u5750\u6807\u7cfb**\uff1a\n\n```markdown\n# [TEXT]\n\n\u8bf7\u7ed9\u51fa\u8fd9\u6bb5\u6587\u672c\u5728\u4ee5\u4e0b10\u4e2a\u6982\u5ff5\u8f74\u4e0a\u7684\u5750\u6807\uff08-5\u5230+5\uff09\uff1a\n\n1. \u5177\u4f53 \u2190\u2192 \u62bd\u8c61\n2. \u63cf\u8ff0 \u2190\u2192 \u89c4\u8303\n3. \u5206\u6790 \u2190\u2192 \u7efc\u5408\n4. \u9759\u6001 \u2190\u2192 \u52a8\u6001\n5. \u5c40\u90e8 \u2190\u2192 \u5168\u5c40\n6. \u7ecf\u9a8c \u2190\u2192 \u5f00\u653e\n\n\u8f93\u51fa\u683c\u5f0f\uff1a[x1, x2, x3, x4, x5, x6, x7, x8, x9, x10]\n```\n\n\u8fd9\u6837\uff0c\u6bcf\u4e2a\u6587\u672c\u5757\u5c31\u6709\u4e86\u4e00\u4e2a**10\u7ef4\u6982\u5ff5\u5750\u6807**\uff0c\u53ef\u4ee5\u76f4\u63a5\u8ba1\u7b97\u8ddd\u79bb\uff01\n\n---\n\n### \u6700\u4f18\u7b56\u7565\uff1aLLM\u5f15\u5bfc\u7684\u6982\u5ff5\u56fe\u904d\u5386\n\n```markdown\n# \u6982\u5ff5\u56fe\u904d\u5386Prompt\n\n\u4f60\u662f\u4e00\u4e2a\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\u63a2\u7d22\u5668\u3002\u4f60\u7684\u4efb\u52a1\u662f\u627e\u51fa\u4e0e\u7ed9\u5b9a\u6587\u672c\u76f8\u5173\u7684\u6240\u6709\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u3002\n\n**\u89c4\u5219**\uff1a\n1. \u4efb\u52a1\n\u627e\u51fa\u4e0e\u4ee5\u4e0b\u6587\u672c\u8bed\u4e49\u8ddd\u79bb\u22643\u7684\u6240\u6709\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u3002\n\n## \u6587\u672c\n[TEXT]\n\n## \u6b65\u9aa4\n\n### Step 1: \u6838\u5fc3\u951a\u70b9\n\u8fd9\u6bb5\u6587\u672c\u6700\u6838\u5fc3\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u662f\u4ec0\u4e48\uff1f\uff081-3\u4e2a\uff09\n\n### Step 2: \u8f90\u5c04\u63a2\u6d4b\n\u5bf9\u4e8e\u6bcf\u4e2a\u6838\u5fc3\u6982\u5ff5\uff0c\u6cbf\u4ee5\u4e0b\u65b9\u5411\u63a2\u6d4b\uff1a\n- \u4e0a\u4f4d\u6982\u5ff5\uff08\u66f4\u62bd\u8c61\uff09\u2192 \u76f8\u5173\u5417\uff1f\u8ddd\u79bb\u591a\u5c11\uff1f\n- \u4e0b\u4f4d\u6982\u5ff5\uff08\u66f4\u5177\u4f53\uff09\u2192 \u76f8\u5173\u5417\uff1f\u8ddd\u79bb\u591a\u5c11\uff1f\n- \u76f8\u90bb\u6982\u5ff5\uff08\u540c\u5c42\u4e0d\u540c\uff09\u2192 \u76f8\u5173\u5417\uff1f\u8ddd\u79bb\u591a\u5c11\uff1f\n- \u4ea4\u53c9\u6982\u5ff5\uff08\u4e0d\u540c\u7ef4\u5ea6\uff09\u2192 \u76f8\u5173\u5417\uff1f\u8ddd\u79bb\u591a\u5c11\uff1f\n\n### Step 3: \u8fb9\u754c\u5224\u5b9a\n\u5bf9Step 2\u4e2d\u8ddd\u79bb\u22643\u7684\u65b0\u6982\u5ff5\uff0c\u91cd\u590dStep 2\u3002\n\u5bf9\u8ddd\u79bb>5\u7684\u6982\u5ff5\uff0c\u505c\u6b62\u8be5\u65b9\u5411\u7684\u63a2\u7d22\u3002\n\n### Step 4: \u6c47\u603b\n\u5217\u51fa\u6240\u6709\u8ddd\u79bb\u22643\u7684\u9ad8\u9636\u6982\u5ff5\u3002\n\n## \u8f93\u51fa\u683c\u5f0f\n{\n  \"core_concepts\": [...],\n  \"discovered_concepts\": [\n    {\"concept\": \"xxx\", \"distance\": 2, \"path\": \"\u6838\u5fc3\u6982\u5ff5A \u2192 \u4e0a\u4f4d \u2192 xxx\"},\n    ...\n  ],\n  \"pruned_directions\": [...]\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### **LLM\u77e5\u9053\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4\u7684\u62d3\u6251\u7ed3\u6784**\uff08\u54ea\u4e9b\u6982\u5ff5\u662f\u90bb\u5c45\uff09\n2. **LLM\u80fd\u8bc4\u4f30\u8ddd\u79bb**\uff08\u7ed9\u5b9a\u6587\u672c\u4e0e\u6982\u5ff5\u7684\u76f8\u5173\u6027\uff09\n3. **\u6211\u4eec\u7528\u6709\u9650\u6b21\u67e5\u8be2\uff0c\u8ba9LLM\u5e2e\u6211\u4eec\"\u904d\u5386\"\u8fd9\u4e2a\u7a7a\u95f4**\n4. **\u526a\u679d\u7b56\u7565\u4fdd\u8bc1\u6548\u7387**\uff08\u8ddd\u79bb\u592a\u8fdc\u7684\u5206\u652f\u4e0d\u63a2\u7d22\uff09\n\n\u8fd9\u6bd4\"\u751f\u6210\u5173\u952e\u8bcd\"\u9ad8\u7ea7\u5f97\u591a\u2014\u2014\u4f60\u662f\u5728\u8ba9LLM**\u7cfb\u7edf\u6027\u5730\u63a2\u7d22\u5b83\u81ea\u5df1\u7684\u6982\u5ff5\u7a7a\u95f4**\u3002\n\n**\u8fd9\u662f\u4e00\u4e2a\u53ef\u4ee5\u7acb\u5373\u5b9e\u73b0\u548c\u6d4b\u8bd5\u7684\u65b9\u6cd5\uff01**\n\n---\n\n# \u2193\n\u6d1e\u89c14\uff1a\u9ad8\u9636\u6fc0\u6d3b\u662f\u7ed3\u6784\u6027\u7684\uff0c\u53ef\u4ee5\u901a\u8fc7\u5411\u91cf\u63a2\u9488\u76f4\u63a5\u5728\u795e\u7ecf\u7f51\u7edc\u5185\u90e8\u64cd\u4f5c\n
